Q1. Tens digit \(8\) and digit sum \(11\) are fixed. What is the number?
A. \(85\)
B. \(83\)
C. \(84\)
D. \(82\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
8+u=11\Rightarrow u=3
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 83.

\[
N=10\times 8+3=83
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 83 is 11.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(83\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=3
\]

\[
N=83
\]

Fast result: \(83\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_missing_digit_single_rule
---
Q2. If the digit sum is \(9\) and the tens digit is \(7\), what number is formed?
A. \(72\)
B. \(73\)
C. \(71\)
D. \(74\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
7+u=9\Rightarrow u=2
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 72.

\[
N=10\times 7+2=72
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 72 is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(72\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=2
\]

\[
N=72
\]

Fast result: \(72\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_missing_digit_multi_rule
---
Q3. The number \(35x70\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 15; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
---
Q4. Between \(228\) and \(400\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(15\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(14\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 228 is 228.

\[
12\times 19=228
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 396 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 400.

\[
400=12\times 33+4
\]

\[
400-4=396
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{396-228}{12}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{400}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{227}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
33-18=15
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_divisibility_multi_condition
---
Q5. Between \(223\) and \(396\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 223 is 234.

\[
18\times 13=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 396 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 396.

\[
396=18\times 22+0
\]

\[
396-0=396
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{396-234}{18}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{396}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{222}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
22-12=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q6. Between \(122\) and \(294\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(23\)
B. \(21\)
C. \(22\)
D. \(20\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 122 is 128.

\[
8\times 16=128
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 294.

\[
294=8\times 36+6
\]

\[
294-6=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{288-128}{8}+1=20+1=21
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 21.
Hence, there are 21 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(21\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{294}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{121}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
36-15=21
\]

Fast result: \(21\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q7. Two buses leave a stand every \(60\) and \(120\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(114\)
B. \(120\)
C. \(126\)
D. \(132\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
60=2^{2}\times 3\times 5
\]

\[
120=2^{3}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,120)=120
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 120.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(120\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,120)=\frac{60\times 120}{\operatorname{HCF}(60,120)}=120
\]

Fast result: \(120\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_divisibility_lcm_bridge
---
Q8. After prime factorising \(108\), what is the number of positive divisors?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
108=2^{2}\times 3^{3}
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(108)=(2+1)\times (3+1)=12
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(2+1)\times (3+1)
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_hidden_divisor_deduction
---

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(5+1)\times (2+1)\times (1+1)
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q14. After prime factorising \(36\), what is the number of odd divisors?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Count odd divisors only

Ignore the power of 2; odd divisors come from odd primes only.

\[
(2+1)
\]

\[
O=3
\]

Answer Verification
The number of odd divisors is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Drop the factor 2 and multiply (e+1) for remaining primes.

\[
O(n)=(2+1)=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_odd_even_divisor_count

Q15. After prime factorising \(36\), what is the sum of all positive divisors?
A. \(92\)
B. \(90\)
C. \(93\)
D. \(91\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(36)=91
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 91.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(91\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{3}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{3}-1}{3-1}
\]

Fast result: \(91\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_sum_of_divisors
---
Q16. After prime factorising \(18\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(6415\)
B. \(6998\)
C. \(5249\)
D. \(5832\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
18=2\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(18)=(1+1)\times (2+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 18 d=18^{d(18)/2}=18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 5832.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5832\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Fast result: \(5832\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_product_of_divisors
---
Q17. Two buses leave a stand every \(126\) and \(234\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(1474\)
B. \(1638\)
C. \(1802\)
D. \(1966\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
126=2\times 3^{2}\times 7
\]

\[
234=2\times 3^{2}\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(126,234)=1638
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1638.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1638\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(126,234)=\frac{126\times 234}{\operatorname{HCF}(126,234)}=1638
\]

Fast result: \(1638\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q25. Power \(7^{80}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
7\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(80-1)\bmod 12+1=8
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
7^{80}\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 8th term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(80-1)\bmod 12+1=8
\]

\[
7^{80}\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_remainder_after_division
---

C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(25-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
11^{25}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(25-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
1^{25}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q41. Number \(47\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(29\)
B. \(27\)
C. \(28\)
D. \(26\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 4+7=47
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 7+4=74
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |4-7|=9\times 3=27
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |4-7|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 30! = 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{30}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q52. Between \(142\) and \(264\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(18\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 142 is 144.

\[
8\times 18=144
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 264 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 264.

\[
264=8\times 33+0
\]

\[
264-0=264
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{264-144}{8}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{264}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{141}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
33-17=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q53. Two buses leave a stand every \(40\) and \(80\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(82\)
B. \(80\)
C. \(81\)
D. \(79\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
40=2^{3}\times 5
\]

\[
80=2^{4}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,80)=80
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 80.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(80\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,80)=\frac{40\times 80}{\operatorname{HCF}(40,80)}=80
\]

Fast result: \(80\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[
m=36

\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[

\]

\[
3^{1}\to 3^{2}
\]

\[
5^{2}\to 5^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification

\[
N=3
\]

Final Answer

\[

\]

Q88. The number \(176x2\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 16.

\[
16+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+2=52
\]

\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
16+x=21
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
16+5=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+2\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q89. Two cyclic events repeat every \(72\) and \(180\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(378\)
B. \(396\)
C. \(342\)
D. \(360\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
72=2^{3}\times 3^{2}
\]

\[
180=2^{2}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(72,180)=360
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 360.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(360\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(72,180)=\frac{72\times 180}{\operatorname{HCF}(72,180)}=360
\]

Fast result: \(360\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q93. The power \(17^{52}\) is very large. What are its final two digits?
A. \(60\)
B. \(63\)
C. \(61\)
D. \(62\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
17\equiv 17\pmod{100}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 20.

\[
17\rightarrow 89\rightarrow 13\rightarrow 21\rightarrow 57\rightarrow 69\rightarrow 73\rightarrow 41\rightarrow 97\rightarrow 49\rightarrow 33\rightarrow 61\rightarrow 37\rightarrow 29\rightarrow 93\rightarrow 81\rightarrow 77\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 53\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(52-1)\bmod 20+1=12
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 61.

\[
17^{52}\equiv 61\pmod{100}
\]

Answer Verification
The 12th term of the cycle is 61.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(61\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(52-1)\bmod 20+1=12
\]

\[
17^{52}\equiv 61\pmod{100}
\]

Fast result: \(61\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(54\)
B. \(55\)
C. \(53\)
D. \(56\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 3+9=39
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 9+3=93
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-9|=9\times 6=54
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 54.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(54\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-9|=54
\]

Fast result: \(54\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Final Answer

\]

Fast result: \(22\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q97. Between \(189\) and \(368\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(15\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(14\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 189 is 192.

\[
12\times 16=192
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 368.

\[
368=12\times 30+8
\]

\[
368-8=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{360-192}{12}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{368}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{188}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
30-15=15
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

\]

\]

Fast result: \(8000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q111. If the digit sum is \(16\) and the tens digit is \(7\), what number is formed?
A. \(78\)
B. \(81\)
C. \(79\)
D. \(80\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
7+u=16\Rightarrow u=9
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 79.

\[
N=10\times 7+9=79
\]

Q114. For \(19^{48}\), use the cycle modulo \(17\). What remainder is left?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
19\equiv 2\pmod{17}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 8.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 16\rightarrow 15\rightarrow 13\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(48-1)\bmod 8+1=8
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
19^{48}\equiv 1\pmod{17}
\]

Answer Verification
The 8th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(48-1)\bmod 8+1=8
\]

\[
2^{48}\equiv 1\pmod{17}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[

\]

\[
N=3375
\]

Final Answer

\[

Q122. Between \(111\) and \(276\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 111 is 126.

\[
18\times 7=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 276.

\[
276=18\times 15+6
\]

\[
276-6=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{270-126}{18}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{276}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{110}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
15-6=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q145. Between \(101\) and \(224\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(15\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(14\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 101 is 108.

\[
9\times 12=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 216 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 224.

\[
224=9\times 24+8
\]

\[
224-8=216
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{216-108}{9}+1=12+1=13
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 13.
Hence, there are 13 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{224}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{100}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
24-11=13
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q165. The number \(741x7\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=11
\]

\[
11-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
11-x=11
\]

\[
x=0
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=0\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
11-0=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
3^{45}\equiv 3\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(45-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

\[
3^{45}\equiv 3\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q180. Between \(225\) and \(371\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 225 is 225.

\[
15\times 15=225
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 371.

\[
371=15\times 24+11
\]

\[
371-11=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{360-225}{15}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{371}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{224}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
24-14=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

Q200. Between \(136\) and \(235\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 136 is 150.

\[
15\times 10=150
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 225 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 235.

\[
235=15\times 15+10
\]

\[
235-10=225
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{225-150}{15}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{235}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{135}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
15-9=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q220. The number \(336x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 20.

\[
20+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
20+x=24
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
20+4=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

Answer Verification

Final Answer

Q223. When \(7^{72}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(72-1)\bmod 10+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 5.

\[
7^{72}\equiv 5\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 5.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(72-1)\bmod 10+1=2
\]

\[
7^{72}\equiv 5\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\]

\[
N=75
\]

Final Answer

\[
M=75
\]

Fast result: \(75\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q235. Between \(167\) and \(298\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(14\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(16\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 167 is 171.

\[
9\times 19=171
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 297 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 298.

\[
298=9\times 33+1
\]

\[
298-1=297
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{297-171}{9}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{298}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{166}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
33-18=15
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(4320)=15120
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 15120.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(15120\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{4}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(15120\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q262. The number \(707x3\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=17
\]

\[
17-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
17-x=11
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
17-6=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q271. Between \(147\) and \(299\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 147 is 162.

\[
18\times 9=162
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 299.

\[
299=18\times 16+11
\]

\[
299-11=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{288-162}{18}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{299}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{146}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
16-8=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q280. When \(11^{41}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(6\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
11\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(41-1)\bmod 12+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
11^{41}\equiv 7\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(41-1)\bmod 12+1=5
\]

\[
11^{41}\equiv 7\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q285. The number \(45x70\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=16
\]

\[
S+x=16+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q301. Between \(221\) and \(320\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 221 is 225.

\[
15\times 15=225
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 315 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 320.

\[
320=15\times 21+5
\]

\[
320-5=315
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{315-225}{15}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{320}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{220}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
21-14=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 1512.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1512\).

\]

Fast result: \(1512\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q313. Between \(149\) and \(262\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 149 is 150.

\[
15\times 10=150
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 255 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 262.

\[
262=15\times 17+7
\]

\[
262-7=255
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{255-150}{15}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{262}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{148}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
17-9=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q317. Tens digit \(3\) and ones digit \(2\) are used in a number. How much does it change when reversed?
A. \(8\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 3+2=32
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 2+3=23
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-2|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-2|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q327. Two cyclic events repeat every \(32\) and \(80\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(168\)

C. \(152\)
D. \(160\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
32=2^{5}
\]

\[
80=2^{4}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(32,80)=160
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 160.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(160\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(32,80)=\frac{32\times 80}{\operatorname{HCF}(32,80)}=160
\]

Fast result: \(160\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q333. Reduce the power \(7^{83}\) by its remainder pattern. What is the remainder on division by \(19\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(83-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 11.

\[
7^{83}\equiv 11\pmod{19}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 11.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(83-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
7^{83}\equiv 11\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q338. Between \(216\) and \(301\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 216 is 216.

\[
18\times 12=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 301.

\[
301=18\times 16+13
\]

\[
301-13=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{288-216}{18}+1=4+1=5
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 5.
Hence, there are 5 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{301}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{215}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
16-11=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q350. The number \(554x2\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 16.

\[
16+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+2=52
\]

\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
16+x=21
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
16+5=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+2\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 20.

\[
20+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
20+x=24
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
20+4=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

B. \(27\)
C. \(30\)
D. \(28\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(12)=28
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 28.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(28\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{3}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{2}-1}{3-1}
\]

Fast result: \(28\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q363. When \(7^{87}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(2\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(87-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
7^{87}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(87-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
2^{87}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q371. Between \(126\) and \(215\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(15\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 126 is 126.

\[
6\times 21=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 210 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 215.

\[
215=6\times 35+5
\]

\[
215-5=210
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{210-126}{6}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{215}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{125}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
35-20=15
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\[

Q389. When \(7^{43}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
7\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(43-1)\bmod 12+1=7
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
7^{43}\equiv 6\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 7th term of the cycle is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(43-1)\bmod 12+1=7
\]

\[
7^{43}\equiv 6\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q397. The number \(36x41\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=-6
\]

\[
-6+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
-6+x=0
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
-6+6=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

\[

\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification

\[

\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q411. The number \(1x870\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q413. After prime factorising \(36\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(11085466\)
B. \(12597120\)
C. \(15116544\)
D. \(10077696\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(36)=(2+1)\times (2+1)=9
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 36 d=36^{d(36)/2}=36^{9/2}=10077696
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 10077696.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(10077696\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
36^{9/2}=10077696
\]

Fast result: \(10077696\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

D. \(6\)

\[

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 6
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(52-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
2^{52}\equiv 6\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(52-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
2^{52}\equiv 6\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q423. Between \(141\) and \(237\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(16\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 141 is 144.

\[
6\times 24=144
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 234 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 237.

\[
237=6\times 39+3
\]

\[
237-3=234
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{234-144}{6}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{237}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{140}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
39-23=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q434. The number \(7x436\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=20
\]

\[
S+x=20+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

(62-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[

Answer Verification

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(62-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

\[
M=5
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q449. Between \(199\) and \(321\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(15\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(16\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 199 is 200.

\[
8\times 25=200
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 320 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 321.

\[
321=8\times 40+1
\]

\[
321-1=320
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{320-200}{8}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{321}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{198}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
40-24=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q450. Two cyclic events repeat every \(48\) and \(120\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(252\)
B. \(264\)
C. \(228\)
D. \(240\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
48=2^{4}\times 3
\]

\[
120=2^{3}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(48,120)=240
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 240.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(240\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(48,120)=\frac{48\times 120}{\operatorname{HCF}(48,120)}=240
\]

Fast result: \(240\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[

\]

Q472. Between \(153\) and \(236\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 153 is 153.

\[
9\times 17=153
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 234 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 236.

\[
236=9\times 26+2
\]

\[
236-2=234
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{234-153}{9}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{236}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{152}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
26-16=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q493. The number \(47x26\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=19
\]

\[
S+x=19+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
19+x=27
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
19+8=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
12\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 6
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(49-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
12^{49}\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(49-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

\[
2^{49}\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_expression_last_digit
---
Q498. For \(76\), what is the total number of digits?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Bound the number by powers of 10

\[
10^{1}\le 76<10^{2}
\]

Step 2: Read the digit count

Hence, the number has 2 digits.

\[
d=2
\]

Answer Verification
Digit count = 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Count digits from the place-value bounds.

\[
10^{1}\le n<10^{2}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_number_of_digits
---
Q499. If the digit sum is \(10\) and the tens digit is \(7\), what number is formed?
A. \(73\)
B. \(74\)
C. \(72\)
D. \(75\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
7+u=10\Rightarrow u=3
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 73.

\[
N=10\times 7+3=73
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 73 is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(73\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=3
\]

\[
N=73
\]

Fast result: \(73\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_unknown_digit_equation

Q500. For the factorial \(75!\), what count is obtained by tracking prime factor \(5\)?
A. \(20\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 5 in n!

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
15+3=18
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 5 dividing 75! is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_highest_power_dividing
